\begin{Indent}\textbf{ :\+ pool name to be created}\par
{\em zs\+\_\+create\+\_\+pool -\/ Creates an allocation pool to work from.

This function must be called before anything when using the zsmalloc allocator.

On success, a pointer to the newly created pool is returned, otherwise NULL.

Function Description:\+ Creates a new zsmalloc pool. It initializes all size classes, sets up statistics, and registers a shrinker. Returns the pool on success or NULL on failure. }\begin{DoxyCompactItemize}

\+\#define MAX\+\_\+\+POSSIBLE\+\_\+\+PHYSMEM\+\_\+\+BITS~BITS\+\_\+\+PER\+\_\+\+LONG}

Object location ($<$\+PFN$>$, $<$obj\+\_\+idx$>$) is encoded as a single (unsigned long) handle value.

Note that object index $<$obj\+\_\+idx$>$ starts from 0.

This is made more complicated by various memory models and PAE. If this definition of MAX\+\_\+\+PHYSMEM\+\_\+\+BITS is used, OBJ\+\_\+\+INDEX\+\_\+\+BITS will just be PAGE\+\_\+\+SHIFT

\Hypertarget{zsmalloc_8c_a6dffdc9a5deb8dfdbf4359962e99e39c}\index{zsmalloc.c@{zsmalloc.c}!ZS\_SIZE\_CLASS\_DELTA@{ZS\_SIZE\_CLASS\_DELTA}}
\index{ZS\_SIZE\_CLASS\_DELTA@{ZS\_SIZE\_CLASS\_DELTA}!zsmalloc.c@{zsmalloc.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{ZS\_SIZE\_CLASS\_DELTA}{ZS\_SIZE\_CLASS\_DELTA}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{zsmalloc_8c_a6dffdc9a5deb8dfdbf4359962e99e39c} 
\+\#define ZS\+\_\+\+SIZE\+\_\+\+CLASS\+\_\+\+DELTA~(PAGE\+\_\+\+SIZE $>$$>$ \doxymbox{\hyperlink{zsmalloc_8c_a4540995708a9e324f9fb5c60f249a054}{CLASS\+\_\+\+BITS}})}

On systems with 4K page size, this gives 255 size classes! There is a trade-\/off here:\+
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item Large number of size classes is potentially wasteful as free page are spread across these classes
\item Small number of size classes causes large internal fragmentation
\item Probably its better to use specific size classes (empirically determined). NOTE:\+ all those class sizes must be set as multiple of ZS\+\_\+\+ALIGN to make sure \doxylink{structlink__free}{link\+\_\+free} itself never has to span 2 pages.
\end{DoxyItemize}

ZS\+\_\+\+MIN\+\_\+\+ALLOC\+\_\+\+SIZE and ZS\+\_\+\+SIZE\+\_\+\+CLASS\+\_\+\+DELTA must be multiple of ZS\+\_\+\+ALIGN (reason above)

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{zsmalloc_8c_a8312726d62abe6eac9b2c617b46d83c8} 
enum \doxymbox{\hyperlink{zsmalloc_8c_a8312726d62abe6eac9b2c617b46d83c8}{class\+\_\+stat\+\_\+type}}}

Enumeration Description:\+ This enumeration defines the statistic types tracked for each size class. It includes the 12 fullness group counters (from NR\+\_\+\+FULLNESS\+\_\+\+GROUPS), plus ZS\+\_\+\+OBJS\+\_\+\+ALLOCATED (total objects allocated in the class) and ZS\+\_\+\+OBJS\+\_\+\+INUSE (objects currently in use). These statistics are used to track memory usage, fragmentation, and compaction effectiveness within each size class. \begin{DoxyEnumFields}[2]{Enumerator}

\Hypertarget{zsmalloc_8c_ad49ab2576a238ebefe966398e04fc07c}\index{zsmalloc.c@{zsmalloc.c}!fullness\_group@{fullness\_group}}
\index{fullness\_group@{fullness\_group}!zsmalloc.c@{zsmalloc.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{fullness\_group}{fullness\_group}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{zsmalloc_8c_ad49ab2576a238ebefe966398e04fc07c} 
enum \doxymbox{\hyperlink{zsmalloc_8c_ad49ab2576a238ebefe966398e04fc07c}{fullness\+\_\+group}}}

Pages are distinguished by the ratio of used memory (that is the ratio of -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}inuse objects to all objects that page can store). For example, INUSE\+\_\+\+RATIO\+\_\+10 means that the ratio of used objects is \texorpdfstring{$>$}{>} 0\% and \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}= 10\%.

The number of fullness groups is not random. It allows us to keep difference between the least busy page in the group (minimum permitted number of -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}inuse objects) and the most busy page (maximum permitted number of -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}inuse objects) at a reasonable value.

Enumeration Description:\+ This enumeration defines the fullness groups for zspages, based on the ratio of used objects to total objects in the zspage. ZS\+\_\+\+INUSE\+\_\+\+RATIO\+\_\+0 means 0\% used (empty), ZS\+\_\+\+INUSE\+\_\+\+RATIO\+\_\+10 means \texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}0\% to ≤10\% used, and so on up to ZS\+\_\+\+INUSE\+\_\+\+RATIO\+\_\+99 (≤99\% used) and ZS\+\_\+\+INUSE\+\_\+\+RATIO\+\_\+100 (100\% full). These groups are used to classify zspages so that allocation can find a zspage with appropriate free space, and the number of groups is chosen to keep the difference between the least and most busy pages in each group reasonable. \begin{DoxyEnumFields}[2]{Enumerator}

\Hypertarget{zsmalloc_8c_a84d6cd29e0dd6514d4b73e190c84d4ae}\index{zsmalloc.c@{zsmalloc.c}!fix\_fullness\_group@{fix\_fullness\_group}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{fix\_fullness\_group()}{fix\_fullness\_group()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{zsmalloc_8c_a84d6cd29e0dd6514d4b73e190c84d4ae} 
int fix\+\_\+fullness\+\_\+group (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structsize__class}{size\+\_\+class}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{class}{, }\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structzspage}{zspage}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{zspage}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

Each size class maintains zspages in different fullness groups depending on the number of live objects they contain. When allocating or freeing objects, the fullness status of the page can change, for instance, from INUSE\+\_\+\+RATIO\+\_\+80 to INUSE\+\_\+\+RATIO\+\_\+70 when freeing an object. This function checks if such a status change has occurred for the given page and accordingly moves the page from the list of the old fullness group to that of the new fullness group.

Function Description:\+ Updates a zspage\textquotesingle{}s fullness group when its inuse count changes. It removes the zspage from its old group and inserts it into the correct new group. Returns the new fullness group.

zsmalloc divides the pool into various size classes where each class maintains a list of zspages where each zspage is divided into equal sized chunks. Each allocation falls into one of these classes depending on its size. This function returns index of the size class which has chunk size big enough to hold the given size.

Function Description:\+ Returns the index of the size class that can accommodate a given allocation size. It calculates the index based on ZS\+\_\+\+MIN\+\_\+\+ALLOC\+\_\+\+SIZE and ZS\+\_\+\+SIZE\+\_\+\+CLASS\+\_\+\+DELTA.

Each size class maintains various freelists and zspages are assigned to one of these freelists based on the number of live objects they have. This functions inserts the given zspage into the freelist identified by \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}class, \doxylink{zsmalloc_8c_ad49ab2576a238ebefe966398e04fc07c}{fullness\+\_\+group}\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}.

Function Description:\+ Compacts a zsmalloc pool to reduce fragmentation. It iterates through all size classes and compacts each one. Returns the total number of pages freed.

zs\+\_\+malloc -\/ Allocate block of given size from pool. @pool:\+ pool to allocate from @size:\+ size of block to allocate @gfp:\+ gfp flags when allocating object @nid:\+ The preferred node id to allocate new zspage (if needed)

On success, handle to the allocated object is returned, otherwise an ERR\+\_\+\+PTR(). Allocation requests with size \texorpdfstring{$>$}{>} ZS\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+ALLOC\+\_\+\+SIZE will fail.

Function Description:\+ Allocates a block of memory from a zsmalloc pool. It finds or creates a zspage of the appropriate size class and allocates an object. Returns a handle on success or an ERR\+\_\+\+PTR on failure.

Function Description:\+ Migrates a page to a new location. It copies data, updates handles, and replaces the page in the zspage. Called by the memory management subsystem. TODO:\+ nothing prevents a zspage from getting destroyed while it is isolated for migration, as the page lock is temporarily dropped after \doxylink{zsmalloc_8c_a4174d2f097f4baff5176e27e1d400890}{zs\+\_\+page\+\_\+isolate()} succeeded:\+ we should rework that and defer destroying such pages once they are un-\/isolated (putback) instead.

\Hypertarget{zsmalloc_8c_ad14f3b69b27d2372f9e4d48b4f8e311f}\index{zsmalloc.c@{zsmalloc.c}!zspage\_read\_lock@{zspage\_read\_lock}}
\index{zspage\_read\_lock@{zspage\_read\_lock}!zsmalloc.c@{zsmalloc.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{zspage\_read\_lock()}{zspage\_read\_lock()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{zsmalloc_8c_ad14f3b69b27d2372f9e4d48b4f8e311f} 
void zspage\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structzspage}{zspage}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{zspage}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

The zspage lock can be held from atomic contexts, but it needs to remain preemptible when held for reading because it remains held outside of those atomic contexts, otherwise we unnecessarily lose preemptibility.

To achieve this, the following rules are enforced on readers and writers:\+


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item Writers are blocked by both writers and readers, while readers are only blocked by writers (i.\+e. normal rwlock semantics).
\item Writers are always atomic (to allow readers to spin waiting for them).
\item Writers always use trylock (as the lock may be held be sleeping readers).
\item Readers may spin on the lock (as they can only wait for atomic writers).
\item Readers may sleep while holding the lock (as writes only use trylock).
\end{DoxyItemize}

Function Description:\+ Acquires a read lock on a zspage. It increments the reader count and allows multiple readers to hold the lock concurrently. Readers may sleep while holding the lock.
